What I established myself:

- `cargo klarch anchors` lists 13 goals homes. The root's is `register@knowledge-architect@goal` (README.md and six files). The 12 Component homes hold no `## ` heading (`grep -c '^## '` gives 0 for each), each with the same two-line "No entry" text.
- The root's home holds 21 goals: model.md 3, built-on-the-model.md 4, workflow.md 6, together.md 2, owner.md 3, adoption.md 3. The files and their order match the list the owner agreed to.
- `cargo klarch check` ends with `PASSED: no findings`. Every reference in the goals home is a a goal reference reference, so rule 8 refuses no citation.
- The brief and the tree agree on every point I checked.

Read whole: the seven files of `register@knowledge-architect@goal`, the review's record, and the section "The owner's rulings" of the analysis. In the analysis I also read the section of every old goal that one of the 21 comes from, and the section "After the rulings". I did not read the milestone document; its words are quoted below as the analysis quotes them.

Abbreviations: "analysis" is `path@knowledge-architect@docs/goal-reviews/2026-11-10-goals-at-the-root-analysis.md`; "rulings" is its section "The owner's rulings". Rule 4 is not judged in this mode; each boundary block gives its material.

---

##### The head of the home, `register@knowledge-architect@goal`

**H1. Rule 2, `asks`, `intent`.** "Its starting point is a documentation structure that it defines, a model that represents that structure, and a scanner." (README.md, lines 5-6). "A scanner" names a means of building the model. No goal uses the word and the head does not define it. The owner's words are "we define a documentation structure, a model representation in Rust and a scanner" (rulings, "The other merges are read again"); the owner later sent "in Rust" to design ("3. design under the scope goal").
Case on which the readings differ: a model built from a project's documents by another means than a scan of the tree, such as through git's object store. Read as an outcome, the head binds a scanner. Read as a mechanism, it does not.

**H2. No rule, `blocks`, `contradiction` (b).** The head defines "**the generic part** of an activity, a tool or a procedure: the part that does not depend on what the project builds" (README.md, lines 31-32). Row 2 of the old `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped` reads: "The generic part of one is the part whose procedure does not depend on what the project builds, ...: it serves each target project that carries out its activity, or is meant to." Its destination is "stays in the goal; the one pass states the definition once, in the head of the root's goals home". The head's definition drops "it serves each target project that carries out its activity, or is meant to". The workflow goal keeps a part of it for an activity ("an activity that only some target projects carry out included", workflow.md line 8). No goal keeps it for a tool or a procedure.

**H3. Rule 3, `minor`, `settled`.** Three shared words have no definition in the head, where `skill@knowledge-architect-goal-setting@goal-entry-shape` puts "the definitions its goals share".
- "a session": used by model.md (lines 24-25, 32-33), built-on-the-model.md (lines 34, 38) and workflow.md (lines 53, 60-62). Only "the main session" is defined (workflow.md line 62). The same goals also say "an agent" for the same actor (built-on-the-model.md line 33: "at a lower cost to the agent"; line 34: "each session gets the same result").
- "a tool": the triage of Q61 in the analysis says "Three kept goals of R-list say "tool", so the definition goes in the home's head". The head holds none. The senses differ: "a model of that documentation, which its tools read" (model.md line 13, knowledge-architect's own), "a tool knowledge-architect provides" (built-on-the-model.md line 34), and "tools and procedures" that "Work in one target project sets up or changes" (together.md line 21, a project's own, "a kind of reviewer" and "an external survey" among them).
- "citations" (README.md line 7) stands for what the goals call "a reference".
Repair implied: define "a session" and "a tool" in the head, or replace them, and use one word for a reference.

Boundary material for the head:
- Asks of future work: other goals either add content to the model or add features that work on it; the workflow and the structure are designed together.
- Requires: a defined documentation structure and a model of it.
- Admits: "tools are built upon the model, or outside of it" (line 13).
- Rules out: nothing by its own words.
- The definition of "a recorded item" no longer excludes a plan document (Q58 of the analysis). So a plan document's entries are recorded items for `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` ("has one home") and for the first goal ("takes a name"). The owner may not intend that.

---

##### model.md

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-has-a-structure-and-a-model`

**1a. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "A reference is a pointer by which one text names a recorded item" (model.md line 9). The project's own use of the word is wider: the old definition read "a pointer by which one text names a recorded item, a file, a directory or a section of a text" (analysis, `documents-stay-current` row 1), and the primer's reference has the kinds `path`, `register`, `skill`. Three other goals use the word under this definition: `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` ("a reference the checker checks"), `goal@knowledge-architect@documents-render-as-a-linked-site` ("each reference is a link to what it names") and `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` ("nothing cited there is missing").
Case on which the readings differ: a pointer to a file or to a section of a skill. Under this definition it is no reference. Then "one readable form, the same in every project ... with one reading" does not bind it, the site need not link it, and the model need not say what it names.

**1b. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "The model judges none of what the documents say. A tool can tell whether what it read is complete and right, and what is wrong where it is not." (model.md lines 14-16). The goal does not say what "judges" means. The old goal did: "To judge what a document says is to report that it breaks a rule on its content, such as a section an entry owes" (analysis, `a-tool-reads-the-documents-with-no-check` row 3). A sibling goal uses the verb with another object: the checker and the tools "judge no meaning" (built-on-the-model.md line 10), while the checker does report breaches of rules on content.
Case on which the readings differ: a reference that names nothing. One agent reads the report of it as "what is wrong" with the read, so it is the model's. Another reads it as judging what a document says, so it is a check's. The old `a-tool-knows-the-read-is-right` ruled it out of the model's answer (its row 8: "the answer naming ... a reference to something missing").

Considered and not reported: the goal holds a structure and a model. A model means nothing without the structure, and the rulings' list gives this goal both, so rule 5 holds. "In Rust" is absent, as the owner ruled. The clause on a tool that can tell whether its read is right is the default the owner took on question 4 of the eight.

Boundary material:
- Asks of future work: one form of reference in every project; a model that every tool of knowledge-architect reads; a way for a tool to know its read is complete and right.
- Requires: a project can declare a kind of entry of its own; each recorded item takes a name; a reference has one reading.
- Admits: a program other than knowledge-architect's reading the model (the default on question 4: "other programs being admitted and not served"); a model in any language.
- Rules out: a reference form a project configures; a model that reports a breach of a rule on content, under the reading of 1b.
- Cases the owner may not intend: a goal is a recorded item (head, line 27), so every goal "takes a name"; a plan item is one too under the head's wider definition.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader` takes its definition of a reference from here (finding 3a); `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` shares "the same in every project"; `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` shares "judge" (1b).

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments`

**2a. Rule 2, `asks`, `intent`.** "The reason of any other decision, where its code or its agent-facing text does not show it, is recorded beside that code or that text and in the message of its commit." (model.md lines 22-23). "and in the message of its commit" names where a reason is written. Changed to "beside that code or that text" alone, the project would still be for a record of what was decided and why.
Case: a decision whose reason is in an inline comment and whose commit message does not repeat it. It fails the goal as written. As a mechanism, a head would close where the reason goes.

The rows of the old goal that stay are all carried (rows 1 to 5). The threshold and the sentence of `records-live-beside-the-code` are absent, as ruled.

Boundary material:
- Asks: each decision that has an entry carries its intent, its argument and the losing alternatives with why they lost; what is outstanding and the flipping evidence are recorded.
- Requires: a recorded decision names the alternative that lost and why.
- Admits: a record kept in an external tracker, now that the sentence on the repository is a design head and no goal; any threshold between an entry and a comment.
- Rules out: a decision recorded with its conclusion alone.
- Case the owner may not intend: the goal no longer says which decision earns an entry, so it is met by a project that records very few decisions in entries (Q3a of the analysis).
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift` names the same three places as "the design the project has recorded"; `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` shares "earns durable text".

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`

**3a. Rule 8, `asks`, `settled`.** "a reference being what `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-has-a-structure-and-a-model` says it is" (model.md lines 31-32). The citation is one rule 8 allows. The same rule says "A goal holds what it needs in its own words", and this goal takes a definition from another goal by a pointer. Four goals use the word. Repair implied: the definition of "a reference" goes to the head with the other shared definitions, which also settles its reach (finding 1a), and the pointer leaves.

Rows: the bound "and where a project may write a reference the checker checks" is kept (row 1). Naming and "one reading" moved to the first goal, as the list says.

Boundary material:
- Asks: an item is cited wherever it bears on other work and a checked reference can stand; a session changing an item can list what to read again.
- Requires: one home per recorded item.
- Admits: no citation at a place where no checked reference can be written.
- Rules out: an item with two homes.
- Case the owner may not intend: with a goal as a recorded item, each goal is owed a citation "at each place where it bears on other work", in code comments included.
- Siblings: the first goal (definition); `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent` ("nothing cited there is missing").

---

##### built-on-the-model.md

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`

**4a. Rule 2, `asks`, `intent`.** "The checker and the tools that list the recorded items, as knowledge-architect ships them, support them and judge no meaning, whatever a project's own checks may do." (built-on-the-model.md lines 9-10). The sentence says who judges meaning (reviews and audits) and who does not (the shipped tools). The triage of Q6 left this to the reviewer. My reading: it is a scope that names its excluded design, a shipped check that judges meaning, so it can stay as a qualifier of this purpose. The analysis records that "no word of the owner in the milestone document states it".
Case: a check shipped by knowledge-architect that compares what a document claims with what the code does, by a model's reading. The sentence rules it out. As a decision in a head, an argument could admit it.

**4c. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "save those the project switches off by a declaration" (lines 14-15) closes a question of how much a project may hold back. The triage of Q11a left the clause to the reviewer.
Case: a manifest field that switches one of the checker's rules off for a project. The goal's words admit it. `design@check@the-regime-has-no-opt-out` refuses it ("There is no flag, no list and no severity", as the analysis quotes it).

Not reported: the example "A mechanical rule, such as that a flag a document names is one the program declares, judges none" is an example the entry shape admits, since without it "judge no meaning" reads as a bar on every rule over content (analysis, row 6). The merge of the two old `check` goals into this one is the rulings' ("stands").

Boundary material:
- Asks: reviews and audits that read documents against code and against each other; a checker whose pass means conformance.
- Requires: a tree that passes meets every rule held for every project and every rule the project declares, with nothing cited missing.
- Admits: a project's own check that judges meaning ("whatever a project's own checks may do"); a breach in a text the checker does not read.
- Rules out: a shipped tool that judges meaning; conformance taken from a reviewer's memory of the rules.
- Cases the owner may not intend: the goal says nothing of when the checker runs, so "at every commit" is design; generated texts are not named.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-add-their-own-checks` (the same "own checks"); the first goal (1b).

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-add-their-own-checks`

**5a. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "It adds them with what knowledge-architect publishes, and with no change to knowledge-architect." (lines 21-22). The owner took the default on question 2 of the eight: "whether a published library API is part of the purpose; default, no, the API is design".
Case: a project's check written as a rule declared in its manifest, with no code built against a published library. One agent reads "with what knowledge-architect publishes" as satisfied by the published checker. Another reads it as code written against what is published, which is the API the ruling sent to design.

Boundary material:
- Asks: a project's own checks run and report with the checker's.
- Requires: no change to knowledge-architect is needed for a project's check.
- Admits: any means of adding a check, under the first reading of 5a.
- Rules out: a check that needs a patched knowledge-architect.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`; `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` ("holds nothing particular to one target project").

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@documents-render-as-a-linked-site`

**6a. No rule, `blocks`, `contradiction` (b).** Old goal, row 1, destination "stays in the goal": "knowledge-architect's documents render as one site, each reference rendered as a link to its target, hosted with the project once it is released." Draft: "A project can host that site with what it releases." (lines 27-28). "hosted" became "can host": a site that is hosted became one a project may host. The owner ruled the reach, "every target project" (question 1 of the eight). The rulings do not record the change of strength.

**6b. Rule 3, `minor`, `settled`.** "The documents of every target project render as one site" (line 26) can be read as one site for all target projects. The title says "A target project's documents render as one linked site". Repair implied: the body says one site per project.

Boundary material:
- Asks: a site build that knowledge-architect provides to every target project. Nothing of it exists (`issue@knowledge-architect@the-documents-do-not-render-as-a-site`).
- Requires: each reference is a link to what it names.
- Admits: any rendering tool; a project that hosts no site.
- Rules out: a resolved reference rendered as plain text.
- Cases the owner may not intend: "the documents" has no bound, so plan documents, open issues and the installed skills are rendered and, where a project hosts the site, published; a private project is owed the same site.
- Siblings: the first goal (which pointers are references, 1a).

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`

**7a. No rule, `blocks`, `contradiction` (b).** Old goal, row 1a, destination "stays in the goal": "a task whose result follows, with no judgement, from the tree ... and that takes several steps or a script the session would write". The draft keeps the first condition ("whose result follows with no judgement from what the project holds", line 37) and drops "that takes several steps or a script the session would write". The triage of Q59a says a draft that drops it "shows the wider reach to the owner"; the record shows nothing yet.
Cases the drop brings in: a task of one command, once the owner has taken it in.

The owner's gate is kept ("and knowledge-architect's owner has taken the task in", line 33), as ruled on question 3 of the eight. "searching, updating and verifying" is the owner's paragraph.

Boundary material:
- Asks: a tool for each task taken in that a tool does cheaper or better.
- Requires: every session gets the same result from the tool.
- Admits: a task the workflow asks and the owner has not taken in, left to the agent.
- Rules out: a task taken in "because the workflow asks it".
- Case the owner may not intend: searching "what its past sessions said" is within reach.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped` and `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow` each have a taking-in of their own (a task, the generic part of a tool or a procedure, an activity). `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` names one of the three objects, "the generic part of a tool or a procedure" (owner.md lines 14-15).

---

##### workflow.md

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`

Conforming. The split ruling is followed: the list of activities and the one clause on completeness are in, and no property of the text. The exclusion of Rust idiom is absent, as the owner ruled on question 5.

Boundary material:
- Asks: a workflow for each activity taken in. Two listed activities have no skill today ("working on a branch under a linear history", "releasing").
- Requires: an agent needs no instruction from outside the workflow and what its project adds.
- Admits: the harness's own mechanisms (the reading of Q38c of the analysis); an activity only some projects carry out.
- Rules out: a workflow that depends on a skill or an instruction outside the installed set and the project.
- Cases the owner may not intend: "complete" beside `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions` reads one way only because this goal defines it; "the list grows" stands beside "the workflow does not grow without bound" (line 33). Both can be met: one is on activities, the other on instructions per case.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions` bounds it; `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` gives the harness.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`

**9a. Rule 6, `asks`, `intent`** (pair with `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`). This goal: "it is not a text that gains an instruction for each edge case or lapse found" and "A case that judgement handles well enough gets no instruction, whichever goal the instruction would serve" (lines 32-34). The other: "What real use shows unclear, missing or wrong in the workflow reaches knowledge-architect and changes the workflow." (lines 55-56). The two can both be met. They compete under a condition the project's use makes possible: a retrospective reports a lapse that judgement handles well enough. The owner's own words name this condition: "the retrospective and the agent text audit can return findings about lots of edge cases, or lapses" (rulings, first ruling).
Case: such a finding, with an argument that an instruction would lead to a better result. Read by the real-use goal, it "changes the workflow". Read by this one, it gets no instruction. "whichever goal the instruction would serve" gives the order from this side only; the other goal's sentence is unconditional.

Boundary material:
- Asks: instructions limited to what an agent needs.
- Requires: nothing positive beyond that.
- Admits: an instruction for a case judgement handles poorly.
- Rules out: an instruction added for each lapse a review, an audit or a retrospective finds.
- Case the owner may not intend: the clause on structure the work does not need is no longer goal text, so a fixed sequence or count is refused only by a head.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`; `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use` (9a); `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-modular-units` (Q26 of the analysis: no ranking is owed, since this goal is a scope).

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code`

**10a. No rule, `blocks`, `contradiction` (b).** Draft: "This goal fixes no definition of quality: what makes code better is learned from use." (lines 39-40). The analysis sends the two old sentences behind the second clause out of the goal: row 2, "What makes code better is hard to define before use, ...", to `design@agent-skills@default-code-shape` as a fact the owner reported; row 5, "and real use corrects it.", to `design@agent-skills@premortem-as-watch-points`. Row 3 alone stays ("So this goal defines no quality:"). The analysis's own draft holds the same clause, so the analysis disagrees with itself. The sibling goal keeps its parallel clause by its row 7 ("what makes a split good is learned from use", "stays in the goal").

Boundary material:
- Asks: what the workflow says about code and tests aims at better code, tests and maintenance tools included.
- Requires: nothing fixed.
- Admits: any shape of guidance; code in any language (the title no longer says Rust; the scope goal gives Rust).
- Rules out: a fixed definition of code quality.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-modular-units` (the split of code); `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-modular-units`

Conforming. The wider wording is the one the list holds, and the body is drawn from the owner's words of round 4 as the analysis quotes them.

Boundary material:
- Asks: the workflow leads to units of one purpose at three layers: parts, the code of a part, design documents by subject.
- Requires: nothing a unit fails alone ("a direction, not a rule").
- Admits: a project of one purpose kept whole; any form of unit at each layer, a module directory included (Q27 of the analysis).
- Rules out: a fixed rule of splitting.
- Cases the owner may not intend: the goal no longer defines a Component or an intermediate Component, so the head that leans on those definitions holds them alone.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code`; `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`

**12a. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "It is the test of how an agent behaves under the workflow." (line 55), beside "This goal forbids no change that is argued to lead to a better result in what a target project implements or records." (lines 57-58).
Case: a mock project built to observe how an agent follows an instruction. Read with "the test" as the only test, the goal rules it out. Read with the last sentence, the goal forbids nothing. The triage of Q29 says the rule on mock runs is a head's and no goal's, so the second reading is the ruled one, and "the test" still carries the first.

See 9a for the pair with `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`.

Boundary material:
- Asks: what real use shows reaches knowledge-architect and changes the workflow.
- Requires: a route from a session in a real target project to knowledge-architect.
- Admits: a change made on argument alone, with no evidence from use (last sentence); a retrospective or any other means of collecting.
- Rules out: by its last sentence, nothing that is argued.
- Cases the owner may not intend: the last sentence generalises the owner's words on D3 ("If you have an argument that the instructions you proposed adding would lead to a better result ... I think the addition should stand. If you have doubts, an issue is fine.") from an added instruction to "no change", and drops the hedge on doubts.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` ("that owner is knowledge-architect's"); `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@session-orchestrates-the-work`

Conforming. The body is the owner's round 1 statement with its "so that", and the last sentence states what the owner left to design.

Boundary material:
- Asks: the main session dispatches agents to carry the work. The installed text departs today (`issue@agent-skills@the-installed-text-has-the-main-session-carry-work-it-could-dispatch`).
- Requires: the main session's focus goes to the design and to reporting decisions.
- Admits: any rule of when, and for which kind or size of work.
- Rules out: nothing by its words.
- Case the owner may not intend: read as a purpose, a harness with a far larger context still owes the dispatch (Q44 of the analysis).
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts` (the exchange); `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` ("reporting each decision taken"). This goal says "the person supervising the work" where those say "the owner".

---

##### together.md

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`

**14a. Rule 5, `minor`, `settled`.** The second paragraph, "Where two purposes of knowledge-architect compete, this one comes first. ..." (together.md lines 13-17), is a ranking, a kind of its own under rule 1. The analysis says of it: "The ranking paragraph is a second intent under rule 5: it means something without the purpose's text." The repair the standard implies is a goal of its own. The list the owner agreed to reads "`goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`, with its ranking", and the triage of Q1 left rule 5 to the reviewer. So the repair would change a ruled list: the dispatcher records it as ruled, or puts it to the owner.

The ranking's words are the default the owner took on question 6: "never before the owner's decisions, the exchange with the owner and the check of the owner's word", without the attention.

Boundary material:
- Asks: what agents build stays within the recorded design; a reversal is made on purpose and argued.
- Requires: a design not kept comes back only by a change that reopens it on purpose.
- Admits: a reversal of any recorded decision, argued.
- Rules out: a decision reversed by accident; another purpose placed before this one, the three owner goals aside.
- Cases the owner may not intend: the ranking places this purpose before `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`, `goal@knowledge-architect@documents-render-as-a-linked-site`, `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code` and every other purpose. It names "the project's owner", so it says nothing of the decisions of knowledge-architect's own owner on asks and on taking in.
- Siblings: the three goals of owner.md (ranked before it); the scope goals bound it by their kind.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`

Conforming. The sentence on quality tools from `setup-brings-quality-tools` is absent, as the reading of the merges has it. The gate is kept.

Boundary material:
- Asks: the generic part of a tool or a procedure a project set up ships to every target project once taken in.
- Requires: what is particular to one project stays there.
- Admits: any form of shipping; a project that keeps its own copy.
- Rules out: a part taken in "because it is generic".
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`; `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` holds the exception for this choice (adoption.md lines 37-38); `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`.

---

##### owner.md

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`

**16a. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "Each decision the agents make that earns durable text, and each choice they make that the owner could have ruled, reaches the owner" (owner.md lines 17-18). "earns durable text" takes its meaning from the design's tests of what earns a head or a comment, and the goal no longer holds the old exclusion "other than an implementation choice that a commit message records" (row 18, sent to a head).
Case: an implementation choice whose reason is written in a commit message. A commit message is durable text. One agent reads the choice as one that "reaches the owner" and owes a line of report. Another reads the commit message as the reaching. A third reads the choice as outside, as the head says.

**16b. Rule 5, `minor`, `settled`.** The second paragraph gives knowledge-architect's own owner "each addition, tightening or removal of what knowledge-architect asks of every target project, and whether and when it takes in the generic part of a tool or a procedure" (lines 13-15). It means something without the first paragraph: another owner and another subject. The triage of Q49 classes it as answered, with the limit "the ruling is by exception on a summary row, not on Q49 by its label". Repair implied by the standard: a goal of its own, or the two members stated in the goals that own them. The dispatcher records it as ruled, or puts it to the owner.

See 19a: after the one pass, no goal says the order of known work is the owner's.

Boundary material:
- Asks: agents decide what argument settles; the owner sees each such decision.
- Requires: a fork and an unresolved contradiction go to the owner; every action that cannot be undone is the project's owner's.
- Admits: a project that reserves further actions; a question on a goal's reading where no argument ranks two readings, under "a choice between shapes that all survive the argument".
- Rules out: an agent's decision the owner cannot see.
- Cases the owner may not intend: the closed list ("and nothing else") is gone, so the goal no longer rules out a question sent to the owner that an argument settles; the taking-in of an activity or of a task is not in the second paragraph (see the siblings of the tasks goal).
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift` (ranked after this goal); `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`; `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` ("asks").

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-s-word-is-verified`

Conforming. It matches the analysis's draft, and no row that stays is dropped.

Boundary material:
- Asks: a check of what a word of the owner assumes, before any act on it, in every activity.
- Requires: nothing is built on an unresolved contradiction.
- Admits: any occasion for the check of a goal's premises ("other than a goal").
- Rules out: work built on a word whose premise the project's own evidence shows false.
- Case the owner may not intend: the installed text asks the check only of a premise the session supplied (analysis, "Met outside the task" of the theme), so the goal asks more than is built.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts`; `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts`

**18a. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** "The owner meets each proposal with its grounding and its consequences established" (owner.md lines 33-34). Old row 4, "stays in the goal": "The owner meets each proposal of the exchange with its consequences". The words "of the exchange" are gone.
Case: a design an agent settles by argument, with no fork, builds and reports under "Decided". Read with "of the exchange", the goal does not reach it. Read without, the owner is owed that proposal "while the owner can still challenge it". `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` admits the first ("Agents decide, by argument"), and the owner said "If there is nothing else I can give other than approval, there is little value in bring the question to me" (rulings).

The sentence "so that the design taken is the one that, under argument, fulfils the goals best at acceptable costs" follows the owner's words of round 2 as the analysis quotes them. "backed by the measurements the agent takes" and "with its grounding ... established" carry rows 6b and 7 of the old attention goal, as the table of lost holders asks.

Boundary material:
- Asks: an exchange in which both sides propose and challenge.
- Requires: a proposal comes with its grounding, its consequences and its rival.
- Admits: any moment for the owner's meeting of a proposal ("before the merge" is a head's).
- Rules out: a proposal whose consequences the owner must work out.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` (18a); `goal@knowledge-architect@session-orchestrates-the-work`.

##### adoption.md

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`

**19a. No rule, `blocks`, `contradiction` (b).** Old goal, row 29d, destination "stays in the goal": "and for the order of known work, which is knowledge-architect's owner's." Draft: "and for the order of known work." (adoption.md line 38). "which is knowledge-architect's owner's" is dropped. The triage of Q51 rests on those words: "row 29d of `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` keeps "the order of known work, which is knowledge-architect's owner's"". `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` lost its own mention (old row 5c, "such as the order of known work", sent to a head). So no goal of the 21 says whose the order of known work is. This sits between adoption.md and owner.md.

**19b. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`.** Two clauses close a question of how much: "Files the workflow installs, the configuration of a continuous integration and scripts kept beside the code are no code it ships" (lines 11-13) and "An edit that a person makes now and then leaves it a target project" (lines 14-15). The triage of Q81a left both to the reviewer.
Cases: a project that keeps a build script in Python, and a project whose owner writes one module by hand. Each clause admits its case by a wording a head could carry.

Followed as ruled: the four statements on asks in one passage (question 7), git and the `claude` harness named as asks (question 8), "knowledge-architect's own repository is one" (the owner: "Since we are ourselves a target project"), and the fourth case of the last sentence (rows 29c and 5).

Boundary material:
- Asks: everything built serves a Rust project that agents drive under a linear history and whose owner chose knowledge-architect.
- Requires: nothing built or repaired only for another project; nothing built only for a state that follows no ask, other than a help into conformance.
- Admits: use in any other project with no guarantee; a target project that does not yet follow an ask.
- Rules out: a mode for another language; support for merge commits; instructions for a project persons drive; a mode of the checker for another harness; narrowing the conditions on what a current target project needs.
- Cases the owner may not intend: a defect that shows only where an ask is not followed is no longer named (the guarantee sentence covers it by inference); the gates asks are not named, so a project's own gate runner is refused only by design (Q75 of the analysis); "its reviews" are agent-driven, beside "easier for a person to review" in workflow.md line 47.
- Siblings: every goal that says "target project"; `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` ("asks"); `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`

Conforming on its own text. The sentence on what a project declares is absent (the first goal holds the declaring), and the form of a citation is in the first goal. See 21a for the pair.

Boundary material:
- Asks: nothing particular to one project in what is provided; each project moves versions when it chooses.
- Requires: a new project is served first; an existing one can adopt.
- Admits: an adoption over several branches.
- Rules out: the cost of conformance used against a check the workflow needs.
- Case the owner may not intend: the pin is not named, so "moves ... when it chooses" is met by any means.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy` (21a); `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`.

###### `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`

**21a. Rule 6, `asks`, `intent`** (pair with `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`). This goal: "A person or an agent adopting knowledge-architect in a target project, new or existing, ... Adopting asks little work of them." (adoption.md lines 52-55). The other: "An existing one can adopt it too, by bringing its documents into conformance. What that costs it is never an argument against a check the workflow needs to work well." (lines 46-48). They compete under a condition: an existing project with many documents. The other goal's sentence ranks a check before the cost. Nothing ranks "little work" against the rest of what adoption asks of an existing project, such as moving its documents into the workflow's homes. "Adopting asks little work of them" is new wording (the old title said "easy"), from the question of Q89.
Case: an instruction that makes an existing project's adoption longer and that no check needs.

The two sentences from the old `create` goal are absent, as ruled ("by one command" is a head; "loses nothing the project wrote" is, in the owner's words, "a small, orthogonal design decision").

Boundary material:
- Asks: documentation an adopter finds where they look, in plain words with examples.
- Requires: no step of adoption that needs the source or knowledge-architect's owner.
- Admits: any number of commands; an adopter who reads the source by choice.
- Rules out: a step of adoption stated only in the source.
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`; `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift` (ranked before it).

##### PART A: Questions of round 2

20 findings as reported, 13 after merging, none dropped. Defaults keep the current text, except T2.

**T1. The head and `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-has-a-structure-and-a-model`** (model 1, whole-set H1)
Question: with the wide definition you took on R2 ("a pointer by which one text names a recorded item, a file, a directory or a section of a text"), does "A reference is written in one readable form" bind every such pointer, or only the reference the checker checks?
Case: a relative markdown link in a README (the goals home's own head holds six); the import line of the root CLAUDE.md; a path in the manifest.
Default: text kept.
If every pointer: the link form is ruled out at a goal's weight, and the model and the site owe "what each reference names" for each. If only the checked form: the definition or the sentence gains that bound.

**T2. `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-has-a-structure-and-a-model`** (model 2, whole-set H2)
Question: the head now defines a tool as "something a person or an agent working in a target project runs, other than the installed text". Is "A tool can tell" any such program, or one of knowledge-architect's?
Case: a project's own gate runner that reads the model. Your ruling on question 4, "4. default", was: "other programs being admitted and not served".
Default, an edit the ruling implies. Current: "A tool can tell whether what it read is complete and right, and what is wrong where it is not." Replacement: "A tool of knowledge-architect can tell whether what it read is complete and right, and what is wrong where it is not."
If you now want other programs served: the text stays, and ruling 4 is reversed.

**T3. `goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`** (model 3; the whole-set reviewer names it as an exclusion not shown to you)
Question: "is cited, never copied" was written by the session's repair S6, on no word of yours. Does it mean "no second home", or "no sentence of the item restated where a reference can stand"?
Case: one sentence of a decision restated with its pointer beside it, which the installed text admits and the root CLAUDE.md does several times; an entry's title in a generated index.
Default: text kept.
If "no second home": such restatements stay admitted, and the words may be reworded or removed. If strict: they are ruled out at a goal's weight, and the primer's restatement rule falls.

**T4. The head, bearing on `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`** (built H-1; the whole-set reviewer lists it as boundary material)
Question: a tool is "other than the installed text" by the session's repair S2. Can an installed agent or a saved workflow be the tool this goal owes for a task taken in? And is a saved workflow installed text at all?
Case: the three audit axes, each run today by a saved workflow; a reviewer agent that gives every entry one verdict.
Default: text kept, so neither is a tool.
If kept: the goal is unmet for such a task until a program does it. If they can be the tool: the definition loses or narrows "other than the installed text".

**T5. `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`** (built C-1, whole-set 4a)
Question: you answered R4: "this was never a ruling, but simply a fact, that a mechanical tool simply cannot judge meaning." The goal now also says of the checker and the listing tools "They are mechanical". Is that an outcome you hold, or a description that forbids nothing?
Case: a shipped check that compares a design head with the code by a model's reading; a project's own check that judges meaning and runs with the checker's.
Default: text kept, read as a description.
If an outcome: both checks are ruled out at a goal's weight, the bound R4 removed. If a description: the sentence may be reworded to keep the fact only, and both checks are design decisions.

**T6. `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`** (workflow 1)
Question: "It is complete: an agent carries each activity out with the workflow and what its project adds, and needs no instruction from outside what knowledge-architect provides and what its project adds." May an instruction an agent needs sit outside the installed text, in the README or a command's help? Your R6 answer: "the changelog cannot be considered "an instruction". I agree on your default wording to fix."
Case: an agent shortens the installed text by moving a procedure to the README.
Default: text kept.
If it must be installed: the first half governs and the second is narrowed. If anything knowledge-architect provides counts: a text no session loads counts toward "complete".

**T7. `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code`** (workflow 2)
Question: "This goal fixes no definition of quality." now stands alone, after R7. Does it rule out a fixed definition, or only say the goal states none?
Case: a design head or an installed instruction that fixes a definition of quality every edited unit must meet.
Default: text kept, read as an exclusion, as the analysis row has it ("stays in the goal: it names the design it excludes, a fixed definition").
If an exclusion: such a head is ruled out, and the sentence may say so of the workflow. If silence: a head may fix one on its argument.

**T8. `goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`** (whole-set 12a; the workflow reviewer raises its premise outside its findings)
Question: "It is the test of how an agent behaves under the workflow." Is real use the only evidence admitted at a goal's weight?
Case: a mock project built to observe how an agent follows an instruction.
Premise to check: the default of R8 you agreed read "with S13 the goal no longer reads as a bar", and S13 was not applied. The record says you were told so, not in which words.
Default: text kept.
If the only test: mock runs are ruled out by the goal, not by a head's presumption. If not: the sentence is reworded as S13 proposed, "It shows how an agent behaves under the workflow."

**T9. `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`** (together D1)
Question: "A recorded decision, or a reason recorded beside code or agent-facing text, changes only by a change that reverses it on purpose and argues the reversal." Does it bound reversals only, or every change? The words "or a reason recorded beside code or agent-facing text" are the session's repair S15.
Case: an agent rewrites a comment to state the same reason for reshaped code, or completes an incomplete reason.
Default: text kept, read as bounding reversals, as the kept row has it ("until a change reverses part of it").
If reversals only: the sentence may be reworded to say so. If every change: each such edit is refused or argued as a reversal.

**T10. `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`** (together G1, whole-set 15a, graded `minor`)
Question: "so that every target project that has a use for it, or is meant to, gets it." Who judges "has a use", and what completes "is meant to"? The words are the session's repair S16. The kept row read: "it serves each target project that carries out its activity, or is meant to."
Case: a testing method taken in, and a target project that runs no such tests.
Default: text kept.
If the project is meant to test so: it gets the part, and the row's verb comes back. If "a use" is the project's own call: the part reaches only the projects that chose it.

**T11. `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`, with `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`** (owner F1, adoption 2, whole-set 16a)
Question: knowledge-architect's owner decides "each addition, tightening or removal of a condition or an ask of" the scope goal. Is an ask only one the scope goal names (git, the `claude` harness), or any ask of a target project, those a design head holds included? R10 set narrow against checker rules and workflow instructions, not these two readings.
Case: an agent, on a settled argument, removes the ask of the lints or of the maintenance crate, or adds a gates ask.
Default: text kept.
If named asks only: the agent decides and reports, the clause adds nothing to "the owner sets and changes the goals", and two heads lose their ground. If any ask: each waits for you, and "a state that does not follow an ask" in the scope goal binds over the same wider set.

**T12. `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`** (adoption 1)
Question: the session's repair S21 replaced the "glue" clause by "apart from the code in another language that a platform it is built for requires". Do you intend what it now reaches?
Case: a Rust game that ships shaders, named by the old text; the HTML and CSS of a web target; a mobile project whose Kotlin or Swift shell is half of what it ships.
Default: text kept.
Each answer sets whether these three are target projects: the first two may fall out under one reading, and the third comes in at any size, where "glue" carried smallness.

**T13. `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`** (adoption 3, whole-set 21a)
Question: after R16 deleted "Adopting asks little work of them.", the title still says "Adopting knowledge-architect is easy". Is ease an outcome, or does the goal ask for documentation only?
Case: a command that writes an adopter's files; an existing project with many documents to move.
Default: text kept, read by its body. R16 put it so: "If no, the goal asks only that the move be explained."
If documentation only: the title may be reworded, and two heads of the root's design home that say "adopting asks little work" lose the ground they cite. If ease is an outcome: the deleted sentence's ranking question against `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it` returns.

Dropped as settled by a word of yours: none. Dropped as a question of when, how much or by what means: none. The reviewers had already left out every point the record settles (R2, R3, R5, R9, R12 to R15, R17, the dropped list of round 1) and reported none again. T2, T7 and T13 are the nearest to settled: a ruling or an answer gives the reading, and the open point is whether the text is reworded.

##### PART B: The goals for ruling

**The head.** "knowledge-architect goals".
Asks: a defined documentation structure, a model of it and a scanner; a workflow; the two designed to work together. It defines nine terms.
Requires the scanner (R1). Admits tools built "or outside of it". Rules out nothing by its own words.
May not intend: T1, T2, T4; "a tool" covers the compiler and git; the run of a dispatched agent is "a session".

**`goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-has-a-structure-and-a-model`.** "A project's documentation has a defined structure, and a model of it that tools read".
Asks: one reference form in every project; a model its tools read, which says whether a read is complete and right.
Requires each recorded item, a goal included, to take a name. Admits a project that declares no kind of its own. Rules out a model that reports a breach of a rule on content.
May not intend: T1, T2; a plan document's items each take a name.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments`.** "A project's documentation records its design, with the intent and the arguments behind it".
Asks: decisions, what is outstanding and the evidence that would flip a decision are recorded.
Requires an entry to carry the alternatives that lost. Admits any threshold between an entry and a comment. Rules out an entry with its conclusion alone.
May not intend: "any other decision" reaches every implementation choice whose code does not show its reason; an entry owes alternatives where none was weighed.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@records-reach-their-reader`.** "A recorded item reaches the work it bears on".
Asks: each item stated once and cited wherever it bears and a checked reference can stand.
Requires a session changing an item to be able to list what to read again. Admits no citation where no checked reference can be written. Rules out an item with two homes.
May not intend: T3; each goal owed a citation wherever it bears, code comments included.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`.** "A target project's documentation stays consistent with its code and with itself".
Asks: reviews and audits judge meaning; a pass of the checker means conformance.
Requires a tree with a missing cited thing to fail. Admits a rule switched off by a declaration. Rules out conformance taken from a reviewer's memory.
May not intend: T5; "nothing cited there is missing" now covers files, directories and sections.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@projects-add-their-own-checks`.** "A target project adds checks of its own, which run with the checker's".
Asks: a project adds a check with no change to knowledge-architect.
Requires its findings to report with the checker's. Admits any form of the means, a rule in the manifest included. Rules out a check that needs a patched knowledge-architect.
May not intend: a check that needs the project's build is still owed a place in the checker's run; T5, second case.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@documents-render-as-a-linked-site`.** "A target project's documents render as one linked site".
Asks: a render for every target project; none exists today.
Requires each reference to be a link to what it names. Admits a project that never hosts. Rules out a render for knowledge-architect's repository only.
May not intend: links to code files and directories under the wide definition; plan documents and open issues render and, where hosted, are published.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`.** "A task that a tool does at a lower cost, or with a better result, is done by a tool knowledge-architect provides, once knowledge-architect's owner has taken the task in".
Asks: a tool for each task taken in.
Requires the same result for every session. Admits a task never taken in. Rules out a task counted as taken in because the workflow asks it.
May not intend: T4; every task an agent carries out is a candidate, a benchmark run or a refactoring included; a task of one step.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`.** "AI agents get a complete workflow for each activity of a target project whose generic part knowledge-architect's owner has taken in".
Asks: instructions for "working on a branch under a linear history" and "releasing", which no installed skill holds.
Requires every listed activity covered. Admits a pointer to the changelog's Migration entries. Rules out an installed instruction that defers to a plugin's.
May not intend: T6; the brief a main session writes for a dispatched agent is named on neither side.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`.** "The workflow states what an agent needs to carry out the activities it covers, and leaves every other case to the agent's judgement".
Asks: an instruction enters only where an agent needs it. It bounds, and builds nothing.
Requires nothing built. Admits an instruction for a case judgement handles poorly. Rules out an instruction added because one session lapsed once.
May not intend: it also bounds instructions that serve the drift goal and the three owner goals.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-leads-to-better-code`.** "The workflow leads agents to write better code in a target project".
Asks: what the workflow says on code and tests aims at better code, tests and maintenance tools included.
Requires the workflow to say something on code. Admits any shape of guidance. Rules out a fixed definition of quality, on one reading.
May not intend: T7; the aim reaches non-Rust code and scripts; the goal no longer says guidance is corrected by use.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@projects-are-split-into-modular-units`.** "The workflow leads a target project to be split into modular units, each with one purpose".
Asks: the workflow moves a project toward units of one purpose, at three layers.
Requires the workflow to say something that leads there. Admits a project of one purpose kept whole. Rules out a rule of splitting that a unit fails alone.
May not intend: it grounds heads on code inside one crate and on design documents by subject.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@the-workflow-improves-through-real-use`.** "The workflow improves through real use".
Asks: what real use shows wrong reaches knowledge-architect and changes the workflow.
Requires a route from any target project, one you do not own included. Admits any means of collecting. Rules out, on one reading, evidence other than real use.
May not intend: T8; a session with no supervising person is no real use; one dispatched agent's run counts as real use.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@session-orchestrates-the-work`.** "The main session orchestrates agents that carry the work, and keeps its focus on the design and the decisions".
Asks: more dispatch than the installed text has today.
Requires work carried by dispatched agents and acted on. Admits the main session reading one known file itself. Rules out a main session that carries all the work as the standing shape.
May not intend: "reporting each decision taken" is wider than R11's words; a harness with a far larger context still owes the dispatch.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@agents-work-without-drift`.** "Agents work autonomously without drifting from the design recorded in their project".
Asks: less supervision, with work inside the goals, the decisions and the recorded reasons; it ranks first.
Requires a reversal made on purpose and argued. Admits any place for the argument. Rules out a silent reversal.
May not intend: T9; a reason held only in a commit message is in neither list; the ranking yields to the project's owner, not to knowledge-architect's.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`.** "The generic part of a tool or a procedure that a target project sets up or changes is shipped by knowledge-architect once knowledge-architect's owner takes it in".
Asks: the generic part of a project's tool or procedure ships once taken in.
Requires a gate taken in to ship for other projects. Admits a generic part never taken in. Rules out shipping a part because it is generic.
May not intend: T10; a tool a project set up now falls under this goal and the tasks goal, each with its own taking in.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`.** "The owner decides what no argument settles, and sees what the agents decided".
Asks: each question goes to the authority that answers it, and every agent decision is visible.
Requires a choice between readings of a goal that survive the argument to go to the owner. Admits a default taken and reported. Rules out an irreversible action on argument alone.
May not intend: T11; every choice with a reason in a commit message "enters the record" and "reaches the owner"; "changes the goals" reaches a rewrap or a slug rename.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-s-word-is-verified`.** "Agents verify the owner's word before acting on it, and build nothing on a contradiction".
Asks: the check in every activity; the installed text asks it only of a premise the session supplied.
Requires the code read before acting on a premise about it. Admits the rest of the work going on. Rules out building on an unresolved contradiction.
May not intend: the check reaches a bare pick at a fork; no check of what a goal assumes.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts`.** "The owner designs with agents as counterparts".
Asks: design decided in an argued exchange where both propose.
Requires the agent to challenge your proposal on its failures and costs. Admits a design an agent closes on its argument outside an exchange (R13). Rules out your proposal built unchallenged.
May not intend: what you are owed turns on which acts count as an exchange, which only heads say.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`.** "knowledge-architect serves one kind of project: a Rust project whose development AI agents drive, under a linear history".
Asks: build and repair for the kind only; never narrow it on what today's projects need.
Requires a check that no current project trips to be kept. Admits a project not yet a git repository. Rules out a checker mode for another harness.
May not intend: T12; T11 over "a state that does not follow an ask"; the last paragraph's exemption names no taking in of a task or an activity.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`.** "Every target project can adopt knowledge-architect, and a new one is served first".
Asks: nothing particular to one project in what is provided.
Requires every list a check reads to come from the project's declaration. Admits a project that stays on an old version. Rules out softening a needed check on an adopter's cost.
May not intend: no exception for knowledge-architect's own tree; a security fix cannot be forced; "served first" has no bound.

**`goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy`.** "Adopting knowledge-architect is easy, with documentation that is clear and reaches its reader".
Asks: each step of adopting written in documentation; the asks of the scope goal are stated to no adopter today.
Requires plain words and examples. Admits an adoption that takes much work, well explained. Rules out a step learned only from the source or from you.
May not intend: T13; "without asking knowledge-architect's owner" reaches an agent in your own project.

##### The head (docs/goals/README.md), where its definitions bear on the batch

**Finding H-1. Rule 4, `asks`, `intent`. NEW WORDING.**

- Head: "**a tool**: something a person or an agent working in a target project runs, other than the installed text".
- Tasks goal: "knowledge-architect provides a tool that does it."
- The record, S2: "Consequence to check: this definition answers B tasks-3. An installed agent or a saved workflow is then no tool of `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`."
- The record holds no outcome of that check. Its "Decided" grounds the definition only on "the triage of Q61 sent a definition of 'a tool' to the head".
- Round 1 put the question to the owner in these words: "The owner says whether installed text can be the tool of this goal." The consolidation moved it to a settled repair, and none of R1 to R17 asks it.
- The exclusion comes from the analysis's draft ("other than the text it installs"), which the analysis says is taken from the head of the old goals home of `cli`. No statement of the owner quoted in the rulings covers it.
- Cases the definition makes the tasks goal reach, for the owner to rule on:
  - a task taken in whose means is a saved workflow, as the three audit axes are today: under the definition it is no tool, so the goal is unmet for that task until a program does it;
  - a task taken in whose means is an installed agent, such as a reviewer that gives every entry one verdict: the same;
  - whether a saved workflow is "installed text" at all. The head defines installed text as "the instructions knowledge-architect installs in a target project for its agents", and a saved workflow is a script the harness runs. One agent counts it as installed text and so as no tool. Another counts it as something an agent runs, and so as a tool.

No other finding on the head for this batch.

- "a reference" is the wide default of R2, verbatim, which the owner agreed to with its cases shown.
- "a session: one run of an AI agent on a project's work" reads one way beside "the main session" of workflow.md, since a dispatched agent's run is a session too.
- "tool" has one sense across the six files under the definition: model.md "its tools read", built-on-the-model.md "the tools that list the recorded items", together.md "tools and procedures", adoption.md "agent instructions and tools".

##### `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-stays-consistent`

**Finding C-1. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`. NEW WORDING.**

- Goal: "The checker and the tools that list the recorded items support them. They are mechanical, and a mechanical tool cannot judge meaning: a rule such as that a flag a document names is one the program declares judges none."
- The owner, on R4: "this was never a ruling, but simply a fact, that a mechanical tool simply cannot judge meaning."
- The record, on R4: "the consistency goal states it as a fact and no longer as a bound".
- The owner's fact is the second clause. The first clause, "They are mechanical", is a statement of the goal about what knowledge-architect provides, and it reads in two directions.
- Case 1: knowledge-architect ships, inside the checker, a check that compares what a design head states with the code, by a model's reading.
  - Agent A: the goal states that the checker is mechanical, a goal binds outright, so the check is ruled out. That is the bound R4 removed, back by another sentence.
  - Agent B: the sentence describes the tools and gives the reason why reviews judge meaning; it forbids nothing, and the check is a design decision to argue.
- Case 2: a check a project adds under `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-add-their-own-checks`, which "run and report with the checker's own", and which judges meaning.
  - The exception "whatever a project's own checks may do" left with the bound.
  - Under A's reading, "the checker ... is mechanical" reaches a check that runs in the checker's run. Under B's it does not.
- The owner's answer says the exclusion was never a ruling, which favours B. It does not say whether "the checker is mechanical" is an outcome the owner holds. If the owner confirms B, the repair is the drafter's: the sentence keeps the fact and stops asserting, as the goal's own statement, what the checker is.

No other finding.

- The second paragraph is old wording. Its clause "save those the project switches off by a declaration" is in the record's dropped list (item 3).
- The merge of the two old `check` goals is ruled ("stands").
- No (b): rows 1, 3, 4, 6 and 12 of the old goal are carried. Row 2's bound left on the owner's answer to R4, which the record holds.
- No text outside docs/goal-reviews/ quotes the removed words: `grep -rn -i -E 'judges? no meaning|as knowledge-architect ships them'` over `*.md` and `*.rs`, outside target/, docs/goal-reviews/ and docs/plans/, finds nothing.

**Boundary block material**
- Asks of future work: reviews and audits stay the judges of meaning; a pass of the checker equals conformance to every rule, in the texts it reads.
- Requires: a tree with a reference to something missing fails the checker; a breach of a rule the project declares fails it.
- Admits: a mechanical rule over content (the goal's own example); a rule switched off by a declaration; a breach in a text the checker does not read; under reading B of C-1, a shipped or a project's check that judges meaning.
- Rules out: conformance taken from a reviewer's memory of the rules; under reading A of C-1, any check in the checker's run that judges meaning.
- Cases the owner may not intend:
  - C-1;
  - with the wide definition of a reference, "nothing cited there is missing" covers a file, a directory and a section of a text (for information; R2 showed it);
  - whether a check a project adds counts among "every rule the project declares" is still not said (for information, already in the record).
- Siblings:
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@projects-add-their-own-checks`: case 2 of C-1;
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@documentation-has-a-structure-and-a-model`: "The model reports no breach of a rule on what a document says", which no longer shares the verb "judge" with this goal;
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`: "where the judgement stays the agent's", consistent with the fact stated here;
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`: reviewing and auditing are listed activities.
  - No pair that cannot both be met.

##### `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`

**Finding 1. Rule 3, `asks`, `intent`. NEW WORDING.** The sentence on completeness now names two different sets, and the two halves move an agent two ways on one case.

- Goal: "It is complete: an agent carries each activity out with the workflow and what its project adds, and needs no instruction from outside what knowledge-architect provides and what its project adds."
- The first half names "the workflow", which the head defines as "the instructions knowledge-architect installs in a target project for its agents". The second half names "what knowledge-architect provides", which is wider: its README, its changelog, the output of its tools.
- Sibling goal, also new wording, title of `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`: "The workflow states what an agent needs to carry out the activities it covers". That title sides with the first half.
- The owner's answer (record, "The owner's answers to R1 to R17"): "R6: the changelog cannot be considered "an instruction". I agree on your default wording to fix." The owner agreed the second half's words. The answer says the changelog is no instruction; it does not say whether an instruction proper may sit outside the installed text.
- Case on which the readings differ: an instruction an agent needs for an activity taken in, which knowledge-architect states only in its README or in a command's help, and not in the installed text. An agent that shortens the installed text by moving a procedure there is the same case.
  - Reading A, from the first half and the sibling title: the workflow is not complete, and the instruction is owed in the installed text.
  - Reading B, from the second half: the workflow is complete, since the instruction is inside "what knowledge-architect provides".
- The old sentence ("needs no instruction from outside them") had one set in both halves, so the question is new. R6 as put showed the owner two cases, the Migration entries and a deferral to an outside instruction; it did not show this one.

**Not reported again**, held by the record: the retrospective and its intake in the list (dropped item 4, on a ruling); the harness's own mechanisms (Part 3, admitted); the brief a main session writes for a dispatched agent read as an outside instruction (Part 3, shown).

**Checked, no finding.** Rule 1: purpose. Rule 2: no built part named. Rule 5: the list names the members of a set, which the entry shape admits. Rule 6: no state meets this goal only by failing another; "the list grows" beside "does not grow without bound" was checked compatible in round 1 and neither text changed. Rule 7: no measure. Kind (b): the split ruling, point 2, is followed; the new clause is the default the owner agreed.

**Boundary material.**
- Asks of future work: each activity taken in gets its generic part in the workflow, enough that an agent needs no instruction from outside what knowledge-architect provides and what its project adds.
- Requires: instructions for "working on a branch under a linear history" and "releasing", which no installed skill holds today.
- Admits: an installed instruction that sends the agent to a text knowledge-architect provides and does not install, such as the changelog's Migration entries; the harness's own mechanisms; an activity only some target projects carry out.
- Rules out: an activity carried out only with a plugin's skill, a user-level instruction, or an installed instruction that defers to one.
- Cases it reaches that the owner may not intend: finding 1, reading B, where an instruction an agent needs leaves the installed text for the README; a text of knowledge-architect that no session loads then counts toward "complete".
- Siblings: `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions` bounds it, and its title bears on finding 1; `goal@knowledge-architect@adoption-is-easy` ("finds what to do in its documentation") agrees with the second half; `goal@knowledge-architect@generic-tools-and-procedures-are-shipped`, `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s` and `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` each state a taking in for their own object, and the last names no activity (shown in round 1, Part 3); `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project` gives the harness and the linear history.

###### Finding D1: rule 3, `asks`, `intent`, NEW WORDING

"A recorded decision, or a reason recorded beside code or agent-facing text, changes only by a change that reverses it on purpose and argues the reversal." (together.md lines 9 to 11)

The sentence reads two ways on a change that reverses nothing.

- Reading A: it bounds reversals. A decision or a reason is turned around only on purpose and with an argument. A change that leaves what was decided standing is not spoken of.
- Reading B: it bounds every change. The only change a decision or a reason admits is a reversal, so a rewording, a completion or an extension is either not made or is argued as a reversal.

Cases on which the readings differ:

- an agent reshapes a function and rewrites the comment beside it so that the same reason is stated for the new shape;
- an agent completes a reason in a comment that it finds incomplete, and contradicts nothing;
- an agent adds to a recorded decision a member its argument already covers.

Under A the agent makes each change. Under B it leaves the text as it is, or writes an argued reversal of something it does not reverse.

What bears on it:

- the kept row 2 of the old goal reads "as it stands until a change reverses part of it through the workflow's procedure", which speaks of reversals only, and the sentence before it in the goal, "What they build stays within the design the project has recorded", is met by a change that contradicts nothing. Both point to A;
- S15 widened the sentence from "A recorded decision" to the reasons beside code and agent-facing text. A comment is edited at most changes of its code, so reading B reaches far more acts than it did on the sentence round 1 read;
- S15 is the session's repair. The record holds no word of the owner on its wording.

If the dispatcher takes the kept row as fixing reading A, the route is `settled`, and the repair is a wording that names the reversal as what is bounded.

Checked on the same paragraph, no finding: "A design the project did not keep comes back only by a change that reopens it on purpose" (old wording) asks no argument, where the sentence before it does. A design not kept that comes back replaces the decision taken in its place, so the sentence before reaches it and owes the argument. The two readings differ only for a design not kept that no standing decision replaced.

###### Boundary block material

- **Asks of future work:** what agents build in a target project stays within its goals, its recorded decisions and the reasons recorded beside its code and its agent-facing text, with less supervision. Where another purpose competes with this one, this one comes first, except against the project's owner's decisions, the exchange with that owner and the check of that owner's word.
- **Requires:**
  - a change that contradicts a recorded decision reverses it on purpose and argues the reversal, or is not built;
  - a change that defeats a reason written in a comment beside code, or beside a project's own agent-facing text, argues against that reason (new with S15);
  - a design the project did not keep returns only by a reopening made on purpose.
- **Admits:**
  - any means of catching a departure, and any place where a reversal is argued;
  - any amount of supervision left;
  - an agent reversing a decision on its argument, within what `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` gives the owner.
- **Rules out:**
  - a silent reversal of a decision or of a reason beside code;
  - a gain in autonomy that takes a decision from the project's owner, or skips the exchange or the check of the owner's word.
- **Cases it reaches that the owner may not intend:**
  - under reading B of finding D1, every edit of a comment that carries a reason;
  - the three already in Part 3 of the record, unchanged by the new text: the ranking is over every other purpose of the 21; a reason held only in a commit message is not in the goal's list, and the new clause does not name it either, while `goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments` has such a reason recorded "beside that code or that text and in the message of its commit"; the ranking names the project's owner and not knowledge-architect's owner on asks and on taking in. For information.
- **Siblings touched:**
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides`, `goal@knowledge-architect@design-with-agents-as-counterparts`, `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-s-word-is-verified`: before it, by its own words. The first now holds the path of change of a goal.
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@design-is-recorded-with-its-arguments`: provides what this goal bounds the work by. No conflict.

###### Finding G1: rule 3, `asks`, `intent`, NEW WORDING

"so that every target project that has a use for it, or is meant to, gets it." (together.md lines 25 to 26)

The kept row 2 of the old goal reads: "it serves each target project that carries out its activity, or is meant to." S16 restored the clause with other words, and two things changed.

- "or is meant to" lost its verb. In the old clause it is "meant to carry out its activity". In the new one it reads as "meant to have a use for it", or as "meant to get it", which says nothing.
- "carries out its activity" is a fact of the project. "has a use for it" is a judgement, and the goal does not say whose.

Case on which the readings differ: a testing method taken in, and a target project that runs no such tests.

- Agent A reads "is meant to" from what knowledge-architect asks of a target project and what the workflow covers: the project is meant to test so, and gets the part.
- Agent B reads "has a use for it" as the project's own call, and "meant to" as that project's intent: the project has not chosen it, so nothing is owed to it.

The same holds for a gate and a project that runs no gates yet. The clause decides which projects the shipped part is built to reach, so the two agents build for different projects.

S16 is the session's repair. The record holds no word of the owner on its wording.

###### Boundary block material

- **Asks of future work:** when a target project sets up or changes a tool or a procedure, the part that does not depend on what that project builds is shipped by knowledge-architect, once knowledge-architect's owner takes it in, for the target projects that have a use for it or are meant to. The particular part stays in the project.
- **Requires:**
  - a gate one target project built, once taken in, ships for the target projects that run gates or are meant to;
  - the same for a testing method or a kind of reviewer, once taken in.
- **Admits:**
  - a generic part never taken in;
  - any form of shipping, installed text included, and any delay;
  - a project that keeps its own copy of a shipped part (that policy went to a design issue);
  - a part shipped that some target projects have no use for, such as a publishing method for a project that publishes nothing.
- **Rules out:**
  - shipping a part because it is generic, with no choice of the owner;
  - shipping what is particular to one project;
  - reading knowledge-architect as a tool for documents only.
- **Cases it reaches that the owner may not intend:**
  - under reading B of finding G1, a part reaches only the projects that chose it;
  - a tool that a project set up and that does a task at a lower cost is now reached by this goal and by `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`, since S10 removed the bound of that goal to "a task of the workflow". Each has its own taking in, of "the generic part" here and of "the task" there. The directions agree. For information.
- **Siblings touched:**
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@tasks-a-tool-does-better-are-the-tool-s`: shared aspect, wider since S10, as above. This goal still means something without it: procedures, the origin in a project's own work, and the particular part that stays.
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@agents-get-a-complete-workflow`: shares "the generic part ... taken in", and keeps for an activity what finding G1's clause says for a tool or a procedure ("an activity that only some target projects carry out included").
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@the-owner-decides` and `goal@knowledge-architect@serves-one-kind-of-project`: hold the same gate. No conflict.
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@only-needed-instructions`: a scope that bounds a procedure shipped as text.
  - `goal@knowledge-architect@every-target-project-can-adopt-it`: agrees, as above.
